Let me understand the problem. This is STLC with CBV semantics, strong normalization using logical relations. The tricky part is `if`-then-else with CBV: `if t1 then t2 else t3` steps t1 if not a value, and reduces to t2/t3 when t1 is true/false. Since if is CBV, strong normalization for if requires that all of t1, t2, t3 are SN. Actually with CBV, if t1 is SN and t2, t3 are SN then if t1 t2 t3 is SN. That's an easy argument: reduction of if is either t1 -> t1' (with if context), or if true/false -> t2/t3.

But careful: ST_IfTrue requires locally_closed t1 and t2 but that's not a problem for SN.

The logical relation approach: Define a unary predicate on terms indexed by type:
- `R_Bool t := strongly_normalizing t` (maybe plus typed closure? For if, we need more)
- `R_{T1->T2} t := strongly_normalizing t /\ forall u, R_{T1} u -> R_{T2} (t u)`? Hmm, but with CBV and `step` on open terms... Let's think.

Actually, standard approach: logical relation defined on closed terms:
- `R_Bool t := SN t`
- `R_{A->B} t := SN t /\ forall v, value v -> R_A v -> R_B (t v)`.

But substitution/opening lemmas: the fundamental theorem needs that for closed t of type T, R_T t. Then R implies SN. But with locally nameless, we do the usual: for `T_Abs`, given `t1` and body `t1` such that for all x not in L, `open t1 (fvar x)` has type T2 in context x|->T1;Gamma. To prove `R_{T1->T2} (abs T1 t1)`, we need: SN of abs (trivial since value), and for all v with R_{T1} v and value v, R_{T2} (open t1 v).

Hmm, but the relation is on open terms with free variables. We define via a context interpretation: for a context Gamma, an environment of values per variable.

Standard approach: define `R T t` where t may have free variables, with an auxiliary interpretation for free variable contexts; and prove "semantic typing": for any Gamma, gamma-valuation sigma mapping each x with Gamma x = Some T to a value v with R T v and SN... Actually simpler: define the relation on terms with free variables (locally nameless has fvar atoms, so we can define R on open terms directly without opening tricks? Actually T_Abs uses `open t1 (fvar x)` and a context x|->T1;Gamma, so we can do semantic typing with contexts directly, using the "free variable" style).

Let me think about the cleanest way that avoids the standard "open" lemmas.

Alternative approach: Instead of logical relation per se, could we use a more syntactic approach? Strong normalization for STLC CBV with if. Could use the standard Tait-style argument but with the reduction relation being small-step CBV.

Why is the logical relation needed at all? Because SN isn't preserved by application: `t1 t2` SN if t1, t2 SN? No! e.g. `(lambda x. x x) (lambda x. x x)` — in STLC untypable but terms can be non-SN if they're not well-typed. But the fundamental theorem restricts to well-typed terms. Actually for well-typed terms, the tricky case is beta reduction: `(lambda: T1, t) v -> open t v`, and `open t v` is a substitution instance. We need a substitution lemma.

Hmm, with locally nameless, is there a simpler route? The reduction relation is `step`, and the substitution when we substitute a value for a variable — because of locally nameless, the T_Abs rule gives `open t1 (fvar x)`. When we have `abs T t` applied to `v`, step gives `open t v`. We need to relate SN of `open t v` to the typing of `open t (fvar x)`.

A common approach: prove SN is preserved under substituting a value for a free variable when the free variable is "fresh enough" and the value is SN. Something like: if for all x, `open t (fvar x)` is SN (for x not in some set? no, for all x), then `open t v` is SN for values v that are SN. Hmm.

Let me think about the logical relation set up carefully; maybe there's a way to sidestep by using a "closed term" logical relation plus a lemma about opening and substitution.

Actually, there's a well-known trick for locally nameless: "if t is a closed term of type T then SN t" — but T_Abs's premise is about open terms with free variables. We need to handle free variables via a context interpretation. So:

Define `sem_ty : ty -> tm -> Prop` by
```
Fixpoint sem_ty (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => strongly_normalizing t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => strongly_normalizing t /\ (forall v, value v -> sem_ty T1 v -> sem_ty T2 (tm_app t v))
  end.
```

So both sides agree! Great: as long as x ∉ FV(t1), substitution is one-pass on both sides and opening inserts v at the same places. Actually let me double check with a concrete recursive proof:

Claim: For all sigma, x, v, k, and t with x ∉ FV(t) [in the sense that fvar x doesn't occur in t]:
`subst sigma' (open_rec k (tm_fvar x) t) = open_rec k v (subst sigma t)` where sigma' = sigma ⊕ (x := v).

Proof by induction on t:
- t = tm_bvar i: LHS = subst sigma' (if i = k then fvar x else bvar i) = if i = k then v else bvar i (since subst preserves bvars). RHS = open_rec k v (bvar i) = if i = k then v else bvar i. ✓.
- t = tm_fvar y: LHS = subst sigma' (fvar y) = sigma' y. RHS = open_rec k v (sigma y) — hmm, here's an issue: opening `sigma y` with v at depth k could substitute inside sigma y if it contains bvar k! That's a problem. E.g., sigma y = bvar 0, k=0. LHS = sigma' y = sigma y = bvar 0. RHS = open_rec 0 v (bvar 0) = v. Not equal!

And values are: abs with locally_closed, true, false. So images contain no bvars. But `open_rec k v (sigma y)` with sigma y bvar-free gives `sigma y` — since open_rec only changes bvars. ✓. `SN_app_abs : forall T B v, SN v -> SN (open B v) -> SN (tm_app (tm_abs T B) v)`.

Proof: We need a strong induction. Formulate: prove `forall v, SN v -> forall T B, SN (open B v) -> SN (app (abs T B) v)` by induction on SN v. Case SN_intro v Hv. We need SN (app (abs T B) v), by SN_intro: for all w, app (abs T B) v --> w -> SN w.
- If w = open B v: hyp.
- If w = app (abs T B) v' with v --> v': by IH (Hv v' Hstep) applied with same T B: need SN (open B v')... hmm, we don't have SN (open B v'). The statement "SN (open B v)" doesn't imply SN (open B v')!

Right, this is the classic issue: the standard Tait proof for CBV needs the logical relation where R_{A->B}(abs T B) says for all v with R_A v, R_B (open B v) — and the induction is over the *typing* or over the pair (v is a value with R_A v). Since v is a value, v' successors of v: v is a value so it has no successors! Wait, values don't step (values = abs, true, false, none of which step). So the ST_App2 case with v --> v' is impossible when v is a value! Indeed in ST_App2, v1 is a value and t2 --> t2'. If v is a value, v can't step. So app (abs T B) v has exactly one successor: open B v (given local closure). 

So the lemma simplifies: `value v -> SN (open B v) -> SN (app (abs T B) v)` — because from app (abs T B) v, the only possible step is the beta step (ST_AppAbs requires value v ✓ and locally_closed (abs T B); if not locally closed, then there are no steps at all and SN is trivial). So:

`SN_app_abs_value : forall T B v, value v -> SN (open B v) -> SN (tm_app (tm_abs T B) v)`.
Proof: apply SN_intro; intros w Hstep; invert Hstep:
- ST_AppAbs: w = open B v, exact hyp (need to match the T and body — injection on tm_app; note the rule's `tm_abs T t` vs our `tm_abs T B`: from `tm_app (tm_abs T B) v = tm_app (tm_abs T t) v` we get B = t by injection).
- ST_App1: abs steps to t1' — contradiction with value_no_step (abs is a value).
- ST_App2: v --> v' — contradiction with value_no_step.
Good.

Let me step back and think about the standard solution to this exact benchmark. This looks like it's from the "Rocq benchmark" family (maybe from the "CoqPilot"/"ProofNet#" or the "STLC strong normalization" tasks). The intended solution likely follows the classic route:

1. Define `R T t` as a fixpoint:
```
Fixpoint R (T : ty) : tm -> Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => fun t => SN t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => fun t => SN t /\ forall v, value v -> R T1 v -> R T2 (tm_app t v)
  end.
```
2. Prove: R T t -> SN t (trivial projection, with SN at both cases).
3. Prove substitution/opening lemmas.
4. Prove fundamental theorem.
5. Conclude.

The application case (T_App) is the crux: from R (A->B) t1 and R A t2 conclude R B (app t1 t2).

Standard "semantic typing"/"logical relation" proofs usually have lemmas:
- (CR1) R T t -> SN t.
- (CR2) R T is closed under reduction: t ∈ R T and t --> t' implies t' ∈ R T. 
- (CR3) R T is closed under "expansion": if t' ∈ R T and t --> t' and t' is the unique successor... hmm, standard Girard CR3: if t' ∈ R and t -->* t' ... 

Wait, actually, here's a key simplification: For CBV, our application rule needs: t1 ∈ R(A->B), t2 ∈ R A ⟹ t1 t2 ∈ R B. Consider the strategy: prove by well-founded induction on (SN t1, SN t2) i.e., on the multiset/lexicographic order of SN derivations. Cases:

So we need a nested induction: outer on type, inner on SN w. Let's define L8 and prove it by induction on T, with the arrow case proven by induction on SN w... but SN w is available from R C w. Let's carefully write:

L8: forall T t, locally_closed t -> (forall t', t --> t' -> R T t') -> R T t.
Proof by induction on T.
- Bool: as above.
- Arrow C D: Need R (C->D) t. 
  • SN t: SN_intro, Hall gives SN ✓.
  • lc t ✓ (hyp).
  • for all w, R C w -> R D (app t w). Prove by induction on SN w (from R C w):
    - w given with SN_intro w Hw.
    - Apply L8 at type D (IH_D— wait, IH_D is for type D: "forall t, lc t -> all successors in R D -> R D t"; D is a component of the type C->D, so structural IH ✓).
    - Need lc (app t w) ✓.
    - Need for all s, app t w --> s -> R D s:
      + ST_App1: s = app t' w, t --> t'; Hall t' Hstep : R (C->D) t', so apply its arrow clause to w ∈ R C → R D (app t' w) ✓.
      + ST_App2: s = app t w', w --> w'; need R D (app t w'): apply the inner IH with w' (SN w' from Hw ✓, R C w' from IH on type? — need R C w' from R C w and w --> w': that's L7 at type C... but we're proving L8 by induction on type and L7 also needs to be proven; L7 and L8 can be mutually proven by induction on type, since C is smaller than Arrow C D ✓).
      + ST_AppAbs: t = abs T0 body and w is a value, s = open body w. Need R D (open body w). Hmm, we don't have this. Hall gives R (C->D) (app (abs T0 body) w') for w --> w' — but w is a value so there are no such w'. So Hall gives us nothing usable. We're stuck with the same problem.

So the general L8 is problematic in the AppAbs case. The issue: the relation R at arrow type is "committed" to applications, so for a term app (abs ..) v with v a value, the only way to get R is via the open body.

Resolution: define R differently, perhaps with the arrow clause stated in terms of both open and app? Or define R such that `R (A->B) (abs A body)` is primitive: use a definition by cases on the term:

```
Fixpoint R T t :=
  match T with
  | Bool => SN t
  | Arrow A B =>
      SN t /\ lc t /\ forall u, R A u -> R B (app t u) 
  end
```

Alternatively, add to the arrow clause a special case: if t = abs A body, then use `forall v value, R A v -> R B (open body v)` instead of app. But then app and abs of the same body should be equivalent.

### Definitions

```coq
Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => tm_if (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2) (subst sigma t3)
  end.
```

R:
```coq
Fixpoint R (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => strongly_normalizing t /\ locally_closed t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => strongly_normalizing t /\ locally_closed t /\
      (forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (tm_app t u))
  end.
```

Notation "t ∈ R T" etc. — maybe just use `R T t`.

```coq
Fixpoint occurs (x : atom) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => False
  | tm_fvar y => x = y
  | tm_app t1 t2 => occurs x t1 \/ occurs x t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => occurs x t1
  | tm_true => False
  | tm_false => False
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => occurs x t1 \/ occurs x t2 \/ occurs x t3
  end.
```

Hmm, max_fvar returns an upper bound (not necessarily the max), e.g., 0 for terms without fvars. Define:

```coq
Fixpoint max_fvar (t : tm) : nat :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => 0
  | tm_fvar x => x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => max (max_fvar t1) (max_fvar t2)
  | tm_abs _ t1 => max_fvar t1
  | tm_true => 0 | tm_false => 0
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => max (max_fvar t1) (max (max_fvar t2) (max_fvar t3))
  end.

Fixpoint max_list (l : list atom) : nat :=
  match l with
  | [] => 0
  | x :: l' => max x (max_list l')
  end.
```

Lemma `max_fvar_spec : forall t x, occurs x t -> x <= max_fvar t`.
Lemma `max_list_spec : forall L x, In x L -> x <= max_list L`.
Then x := S (max (max_list L) (max_fvar t1)); x ∉ L (since x ≤ max_list L would contradict x = S(...) > max_list L... need x ≠ any element: if x ∈ L then x ≤ max_list L < x, contradiction via lia). Similarly x ∉ FV(t1) (i.e., ~ occurs x t1).

### The fundamental theorem

Statement:
```coq
Theorem fundamental : forall Gamma t T,
  has_type Gamma t T ->
  forall rho, (forall x S, Gamma x = Some S -> value (rho x) /\ R S (rho x)) ->
  R T (subst rho t).
```
Hmm, do we need `value (rho x)` separately? R S (rho x) gives lc (rho x) and SN, but the commute lemma needs rho images to be bvar-free (lc_at 0). R_lc gives locally_closed (rho x) = lc_at 0 ✓. So `forall x S, Gamma x = Some S -> R S (rho x)` suffices; and "rho x is lc_at 0" follows by R_lc. Good. But in the T_Abs case, we need rho' x = v to be a value (for the arrow clause) — v is chosen as a value u with R T1 u; and rho' = rho[x := v]. We need rho' to satisfy the context condition for `x|->T1;Gamma` ✓ and rho' images to be lc_at 0: rho y lc_at 0 ✓ (from rho's condition via R_lc), and v lc_at 0 from R T1 v via R_lc ✓.

Proof by induction on the typing derivation.

- T_Var: Gamma x = Some T; goal R T (subst rho (fvar x)) = R T (rho x); apply Hrho ✓.
- T_True: R Bool (subst rho true) = R Bool true = SN true ∧ lc true ✓.
- T_False: similar.
- T_Abs: Gamma |-- abs T1 t1 ∈ T1 -> T2, from Hprem : forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2.
  Goal: R (T1->T2) (subst rho (abs T1 t1)) = R (T1->T2) (abs T1 (subst rho t1)).
  Need: SN (abs T1 B) ✓ (value_SN); lc (abs T1 B) = lc_at 1 B; forall u, R T1 u -> R (T2) (app (abs T1 B) u).
  
  First, get the "body property": for all v, value v -> R T1 v -> R T2 (open B v), where B = subst rho t1.
  Pick x fresh: x ∉ L and ~ occurs x t1.
  Define rho' := fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then v else rho y.
  We have Hrho' : forall y S, (update Gamma x T1) y = Some S -> R S (rho' y).
    Proof: update Gamma x T1 y = if x =? y then Some T1 else Gamma y. If y = x: then Some T1 = Some S → S = T1 and rho' y = v, need R T1 v ✓. If y ≠ x: Some S = Gamma y → need R S (rho y) ✓ (since rho' y = rho y).
  IH: R T2 (subst rho' (open t1 (fvar x))).
  Commute: subst rho' (open t1 (fvar x)) = open_rec 0 v (subst rho t1) = open B v. Wait, `open B v = open_rec 0 v B` ✓. So IH gives R T2 (open B v) — but also lc_at 0 of it, hence lc_at 1 B by lc_open_inv ✓, hence lc (abs T1 B) ✓.
  
  Then, for the arrow clause: forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (app (abs T1 B) u). 
    We have Hbody : forall v, value v -> R T1 v -> R T2 (open B v).
    Prove by induction on SN u (R T1 u gives SN u):
    - u is a value: R_back_step at type T2 with t = app (abs T1 B) u, t' = open B u:
      * lc t: lc (abs T1 B) ✓ and lc u (R_lc) ✓.
      * step: ST_AppAbs with locally_closed (abs T1 B) ✓ and value u ✓ — need the term shapes: `tm_app (tm_abs T1 B) u --> open B u` ✓ definitionally.
      * unique successor: inversion on step with hypothesis `app (abs T1 B) u --> s`:
        + ST_AppAbs: gives s = open B u (after injections) ✓.
        + ST_App1: requires abs T1 B --> t1', contradict value_no_step ✓.
        + ST_App2: requires u --> t2', contradict value_no_step ✓.
      * R T2 (open B u) from Hbody ✓.
      → R T2 (app (abs T1 B) u) ✓.
    - u is not a value: use R_intro_nonvalue at type T2 applied to app (abs T1 B) u:
      * lc ✓; not a value (app is never a value) — need lemma `value_app_abs`? Actually any `tm_app` is not a value: `value (tm_app t1 t2) -> False`, by inversion on value. So `~ value (app (abs T1 B) u)` ✓.
      * all successors s in R T2: inversion on step:
        - ST_AppAbs: requires value u ✗.
        - ST_App1: requires abs T1 B --> t1' ✗ (value_no_step).
        - ST_App2: value (abs T1 B) ✓, u --> u', s = app (abs T1 B) u'; need R T2 (app (abs T1 B) u'): apply IH_u' with SN u' (from SN u) and R T1 u' (R_step at type T1) ✓.
      → R T2 (app (abs T1 B) u) ✓.

  Then R (T1->T2) (abs T1 B) via the definition ✓.

- T_App: H1 : Gamma |-- t1 ∈ T1 -> T2 (IH), H2 : Gamma |-- t2 ∈ T1 (IH).
  Goal R T2 (subst rho (app t1 t2)) = R T2 (app (subst rho t1) (subst rho t2)).
  IH1 : R (T1->T2) (subst rho t1) → its arrow clause with u := subst rho t2 and IH2 : R T1 (subst rho t2) gives R T2 (app (subst rho t1) (subst rho t2)) ✓. Immediate.

- T_If: H1 : Gamma |-- t1 ∈ Bool, H2 : t2 ∈ T, H3 : t3 ∈ T.
  Goal: R T (if (subst rho t1) (subst rho t2) (subst rho t3)).
  IH1: R Bool (subst rho t1) → SN and lc.
  IH2, IH3: R T (subst rho t2), R T (subst rho t3).
  Need: R T (if t1' t2' t3') where t1' = subst rho t1 etc.
  
  Analysis: prove by the general "R_intro" lemma? The if term is not a value. Successors of if t1' t2' t3':
  - ST_IfTrue: if t1' = true, successor t2'. Requires t1' = tm_true.
  - ST_IfFalse: if t1' = false, successor t3'.
  - ST_If: t1' --> t1'', successor if t1'' t2' t3'.
  So: use the general lemma `R_if`:
  `R_if : forall T t1 t2 t3, R Bool t1 -> R T t2 -> R T t3 -> R T (tm_if t1 t2 t3)`.
  Proof by induction on SN t1 (R Bool t1 gives SN t1):
  - SN_intro with IH for successors t1'.
  - Case t1 = true: if true t2 t3 --> t2 (ST_IfTrue requires lc t2, lc t3 ✓ from R_lc). Is t2 the unique successor? ST_IfTrue applies (lc t2, lc t3 given); ST_If requires true --> t1' ✗; so unique: t2. R T (if true t2 t3) from R T t2 by R_back_step ✓ (unique successor).
  - Case t1 = false: similarly t3.
  - Case t1 not a value and not true/false: then if t1 t2 t3 successors are only via ST_If: if t1' t2 t3. Use the general R_intro_nonvalue-ish lemma at type T: need lc ✓, not a value (if is never a value ✓), and all successors in R T: ST_If gives if t1'' t2 t3 with IH on SN t1'' and R Bool t1'' (R_step at Bool). But wait: what if t1 = true? Then t1 IS a value (v_true). The cases "t1 is true/false" are values; the "not a value" case excludes them, and then ST_IfTrue/False don't apply (they need t1 = true/false).
    Hmm, but for the "not a value" case, the successors via ST_If: need R T (if t1'' t2 t3) — by the inner induction hypothesis, since SN t1'' and R Bool t1'' ✓, applying the lemma recursively. This works with induction on SN t1.

  So `R_if` needs induction on SN t1, with:
  - true/false cases: R_back_step (unique successor) at type T.
  - non-value: R_intro_nonvalue at type T with all successors from ST_If in R T (using the induction hypothesis).

  Hmm, careful: in the non-value case, could ST_IfTrue apply? It requires t1 = tm_true, but t1 not a value in this case; tm_true is a value, so t1 ≠ tm_true ✓. Could ST_AppAbs etc.? No, if only steps via the if rules ✓.

  But there's a subtlety in the "not a value" case: R_intro_nonvalue requires `locally_closed (if t1 t2 t3)` ✓ from lc t1, t2, t3 (R_lc), and `~ value (if ...)` ✓ (if is never a value).

  Also in the true/false cases, `R_back_step` requires lc of the if term ✓, and it needs "unique successor" ✓ as argued, and R T t2 ✓.

  Wait, one more check: R_back_step requires the successor relation `if true t2 t3 --> t2` to exist — ST_IfTrue requires locally_closed t1 and locally_closed t2 — where the rule's premises are (t1 t2 as the two branches): `ST_IfTrue : forall t1 t2, locally_closed t1 -> locally_closed t2 -> tm_if tm_true t1 t2 --> t1`. So we need lc of the branches (t2 and t3 in our naming) ✓ from R_lc.

  Hmm, but what if T = Bool and t1 = true: R_back_step at Bool: fine.

  Actually hold on, in the true case, is `t2` really the unique successor? ST_If rule requires `true --> t1'` — no such step ✓ (value_no_step). So yes.

Now the general lemmas needed:

`R_intro_nonvalue`:
```coq
Lemma R_intro_nonvalue : forall T t,
  locally_closed t ->
  ~ value t ->
  (forall t', t --> t' -> R T t') ->
  R T t.
```
Proof by induction on T (structural):
- T = Ty_Bool: split; [apply SN_intro; intros; apply (proj1 (H1 t' H2)) | exact H].
- T = Ty_Arrow A B:
  * SN t: SN_intro; intro t'; apply R_SN with (T := Ty_Arrow A B); apply H1 ✓.
  * lc ✓.
  * forall u, R A u -> R B (app t u): apply IH_B (B is structurally smaller) with:
    - lc (app t u) ✓.
    - ~ value (app t u) ✓ (need lemma: values are not apps).
    - forall s, app t u --> s -> R B s: inversion on step:
      + ST_AppAbs: requires t = tm_abs T0 body, but then value t (v_abs) contradicting ~value t ✓.
      + ST_App1: t --> t', s = app t' u; apply H1 t' Hstep's arrow clause to u ✓: H1 t' Hstep : R (A->B) t', so its third component applied to u and Hru gives R B (app t' u) ✓.
      + ST_App2: requires value t ✗.
    - IH_B is the induction hypothesis for type B — but careful: `induction T` gives IHs for A and B when T = Arrow A B ✓. But the IH for B is `forall t, lc t -> ~value t -> (forall t', t --> t' -> R B t') -> R B t` ✓ exactly what we need.
    
  Wait, but there's a circularity concern: in the arrow case, we also need SN of the successors? No, the SN part is done via SN_intro + R_SN of H1 ✓.

  Hmm, actually there's a subtle issue with `induction T`: the statement must be generalized correctly. `Lemma R_intro_nonvalue : forall T, (forall t, ...)`. Doing `induction T` might not give IHs in the right form because t is quantified after T. We can do `intros T; induction T; intros t Hlc Hnv Hall`. Then in the arrow case, IHs are for T1 and T2 as needed ✓.

`R_back_step`:
```coq
Lemma R_back_step : forall T t t',
  locally_closed t ->
  t --> t' ->
  (forall t'', t --> t'' -> t'' = t') ->
  R T t' ->
  R T t.
```
Proof by induction on T:
- Bool: split; [SN_intro; intros s Hstep; rewrite (Huniq s Hstep); apply R_SN with T := Ty_Bool; exact Hrt' | exact Hlc].
  Hmm, R T t' gives SN t' ✓ (proj1) and lc t' but we need lc t: given ✓.
- Arrow A B:
  * SN t: SN_intro; intro s; apply R_SN with T := Arrow A B; apply Hrt'... wait: Hrt' : R (A->B) t'. Need R (A->B) s where s is a successor of t: rewrite (Huniq s Hstep) → R (A->B) t' ✓.
  * lc t ✓ (hyp).
  * forall u, R A u -> R B (app t u): apply IH_B with t := app t u, t' := app t' u:
    - lc (app t u) ✓ (Hlc, R_lc u).
    - app t u --> app t' u: ST_App1 with Hstep and lc u ✓.
    - uniqueness: forall s, app t u --> s -> s = app t' u. Inversion on step:
      + ST_AppAbs: t = tm_abs T0 body → value t, contradicting... hmm, do we know ~value t? t --> t' and values don't step → ~ value t ✓ (value_no_step).
      + ST_App1: t --> t0, s = app t0 u; t0 = t' by Huniq ✓.
      + ST_App2: value t ✗.
    - R B (app t' u): from Hrt' arrow clause with u ✓.
    - IH_B ✓ (B structurally smaller).
    
  Hmm, but wait: IH_B is the induction hypothesis of the structural induction on T. Since T = Arrow A B, B is a subterm ✓.

`R_step` (successor closure):
```coq
Lemma R_step : forall T t t', R T t -> t --> t' -> R T t'.
```
Proof by induction on T:
- Bool: destruct Hrt as [HSN Hlc]; split; [apply SN_preserved... need: SN t and t --> t' → SN t'].
  Lemma `SN_step : strongly_normalizing t -> t --> t' -> strongly_normalizing t'`: inversion on SN t (SN_intro case) → H t' Hstep ✓.
  And lc t' from lc_step Hlc Hstep (proven separately).
- Arrow A B: destruct Hrt as [HSN [Hlc Hfun]]; split; [SN_step ✓ | lc_step ✓ | intros u Hru; apply IH_B with (app t u) (app t' u); [apply Hfun ✓ | ST_App1 with lc u ✓] ].
  Wait IH_B : forall t t', R B t -> t --> t' -> R B t'. Use it with t := app t u, t' := app t' u ✓.

`lc_step`: locally_closed t -> t --> t' -> locally_closed t'. Proof by induction on the step derivation:
- ST_AppAbs: t = app (abs T t1) v, t' = open t1 v. lc (app (abs T t1) v) → lc (abs T t1) and lc v → lc_at 1 t1 and lc v → lc_open 0 t1 v ✓.
- ST_App1: lc (app t1 t2) → lc t1, lc t2; t' = app t1' t2; IH gives lc t1' ✓.
- ST_App2: similar.
- ST_IfTrue: t' = t1; lc of if gives lc t1 ✓.
- ST_IfFalse ✓.
- ST_If: lc t1, t2, t3; IH lc t1'; ✓.

Careful with the ST_AppAbs case: hypothesis `locally_closed (tm_app (tm_abs T t1) v)`; inversion gives lc_at 0 (abs T t1) and lc_at 0 v; lc_at 0 (abs T t1) → lc_at 1 t1; lc_open with k=0: lc_at (S 0) t1 = lc_at 1 t1 ✓, lc_at 0 v ✓ → lc_at 0 (open_rec 0 v t1) = locally_closed (open t1 v) ✓.

`R_SN`, `R_lc` projections: by destruct on T? `R` is a Fixpoint computing on T: `R Ty_Bool t = SN t /\ lc t`, `R (Arrow A B) t = SN t /\ lc t /\ ...`. So:
```coq
Lemma R_SN : forall T t, R T t -> strongly_normalizing t.
Proof. intros T; destruct T; intros t H; exact (proj1 H). Qed.
```
Hmm, `destruct T` unfolds R? `R T t` with T a variable: after destruct T, it becomes the match reduced ✓ (simpl). For Bool: proj1 ✓; for Arrow: proj1 ✓ (SN is the first component) ✓.

`R_lc`:
```coq
Lemma R_lc : forall T t, R T t -> locally_closed t.
Proof. intros T; destruct T; intros t H; exact (proj1 (proj2 H)) ... 
```
For Bool: H : SN t /\ lc t → proj2 ✓. For Arrow: H : SN t /\ lc t /\ (...): proj1 (proj2 H) ✓.

`R_app` (arrow elimination):
```coq
Lemma R_app : forall A B t u, R (A->B) t -> R A u -> R B (tm_app t u).
Proof. intros; exact (proj2 (proj2 H) u H0). Qed.
```

Case HstepL : forall t', t1 --> t' -> SN t' and IH : forall t', t1 --> t' -> lc t' -> R T t2 -> R T t3 -> R T (if t' t2 t3).

Hmm, careful: the IH from the induction principle for SN: does Rocq give `IH : forall t', step t1 t' -> P t'`? The constructor SN_intro has premise `(forall t', t --> t' -> SN t')`, and the generated induction principle replaces recursive occurrences of SN in the premise with P. I believe Rocq's scheme gives: forall t, (forall t', step t t' -> SN t') -> (forall t', step t t' -> P t') -> P t. So IH : forall t', t1 --> t' -> P t' where P includes lc t' → ... Hmm, is the generated principle's P applied to t' inside the "forall t', step t t' -> P t'" or is it "forall t', step t t' -> SN t'" replaced by P pointwise? The standard generated principle for Acc-like:
```
strongly_normalizing_ind :
forall P : tm -> Prop,
  (forall t : tm,
      (forall t' : tm, step t t' -> strongly_normalizing t') ->
      (forall t' : tm, step t t' -> P t') -> P t) ->
  forall t : tm, strongly_normalizing t -> P t
```
Yes, this is the Acc-style scheme ✓. So IH : forall t', step t1 t' -> P t' ✓ with P t' = locally_closed t' -> R T t2 -> R T t3 -> R T (if t' t2 t3).

Then case analysis on t1:

But wait, the IH at type B is about `R_stuck_nonvalue` at B — structural induction on T ✓. Yes! This works:

```coq
Lemma R_stuck : forall T t, locally_closed t -> ~ value t -> (forall t', t --> t' -> False) -> R T t.
Proof.
  induction T; intros t Hlc Hnv Hns.
  - (* Bool *) split; [apply SN_intro; intros; exfalso; apply Hns; exact H | exact Hlc].
  - (* Arrow A B *) split; [apply SN_intro; intros t' Hs; exfalso; apply Hns; exact Hs | split; [exact Hlc | intros u Hru]].
    apply IHT2.  (* R_stuck at B *)
    + apply lc_app; [exact Hlc | apply R_lc with (T := A); exact Hru].
    + (* ~ value (app t u) *) apply value_not_app.
    + intros s Hs; inversion Hs; subst; ...
      * ST_AppAbs: t = abs ... → Hnv (v_abs ...) ✗
      * ST_App1: t --> t' ✗ (Hns)
      * ST_App2: value t ✗
Qed.
```
Note: for the ST_AppAbs case, inversion gives t = tm_abs T0 body (and lc (abs T0 body) as premise) — contradict Hnv using v_abs with the premise's lc ✓.

### Freshness machinery

```coq
Fixpoint max_fvar (t : tm) : nat
Fixpoint max_atom_list (L : list atom) : nat
```

### occurs function

```coq
Fixpoint occurs (x : atom) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => False
  | tm_fvar y => x = y
  | tm_app t1 t2 => occurs x t1 \/ occurs x t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => occurs x t1
  | tm_true => False
  | tm_false => False
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => occurs x t1 \/ occurs x t2 \/ occurs x t3
  end.
```
Note: `x = y` as a Prop (eq). For the commute lemma's fvar case: `~ occurs x (tm_fvar y)` gives `x = y -> False` → y ≠ x ✓.

Hmm, careful: in the bvar case of subst_open, the LHS is `subst (extend rho x v) (tm_fvar x)` = extend rho x v x = if Nat.eqb x x then v else ... = v ✓.

### open_rec_lc lemma

`lc_open_rec_id : forall t k v, locally_closed t -> open_rec k v t = t`. Hmm, locally_closed = lc_at 0. Proof by induction on t generalizing k:
- bvar i: lc_at 0 (bvar i) → i < 0 → contradiction (lia).
- fvar ✓.
- app: IH ✓.
- abs T t1: need open_rec k v (abs T t1) = abs T t1, i.e., abs T (open_rec (S k) v t1) = abs T t1; need IH with k := S k, requiring lc_at 0 t1. But from lc_at 0 (abs T t1) we get lc_at 1 t1 — we need lc_at 0 t1! Hmm. Is that right? If t1 has bvar 0 (referring to the abs binder), then lc_at 0 t1 fails but t = abs T t1 is lc ✓. And open_rec k v (abs T t1) with the bvar 0 references: open_rec increments k under the binder, so bvar 0 (at body depth 0) is replaced only if k = 0... Since k is arbitrary, e.g., k=0: open_rec 0 v (abs T (bvar 0)) = abs T (open_rec 1 v (bvar 0)) = abs T (bvar 0) ✓ unchanged. Hmm, so the claim might still be true but needs a different statement: if lc_at 0 t, then open_rec k v t = t — for the abs case we need to prove open_rec (S k) v t1 = t1 with lc_at 1 t1?

Let's generalize: `lc_at k t -> open_rec j v t = t` for all j with...? Hmm: bvar i with lc_at k: i < k. open_rec j replaces bvar j. Hmm, at depth d inside t, the check is i < k+d, and open_rec has j+d; so we need j+d ≥ k+d, i.e., j ≥ k. So: `lc_at k t -> j >= k -> open_rec j v t = t`. Let's prove by induction on t generalizing j, k:
- bvar i: lc_at k (bvar i) → i < k ≤ j → i ≠ j ✓.
- abs: lc_at k (abs T t1) → lc_at (S k) t1; open_rec j (abs T t1) = abs T (open_rec (S j) v t1); need open_rec (S j) v t1 = t1 by IH with k := S k, j := S j; need S j ≥ S k ✓.
✓. So the lemma `open_rec_lc : forall t j k v, lc_at k t -> k <= j -> open_rec j v t = t`. Also need monotonicity of lc_at.

In subst_open's fvar case: rho y is locally_closed = lc_at 0, and j = k (the current k) ≥ 0 ✓.

Hmm, in subst_open the goal's k changes under binders, but the rho images stay the same and we need open_rec j v (rho y) = rho y for j = the current k ≥ 0 ✓.

Let me draft:

```coq
Fixpoint occurs (x : atom) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => False
  | tm_fvar y => x = y
  | tm_app t1 t2 => occurs x t1 \/ occurs x t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => occurs x t1
  | tm_true => False
  | tm_false => False
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => occurs x t1 \/ occurs x t2 \/ occurs x t3
  end.

Fixpoint subst (rho : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => rho x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst rho t1) (subst rho t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst rho t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => tm_if (subst rho t1) (subst rho t2) (subst rho t3)
  end.

Definition extend (rho : atom -> tm) (x : atom) (v : tm) : atom -> tm :=
  fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then v else rho y.
```

lc_at_mono:
```coq
Lemma lc_at_mono : forall k k' t, k <= k' -> lc_at k t -> lc_at k' t.
Proof.
  intros k k' t Hle H. induction H; try (constructor; lia); 
  ...
Qed.
```
Hmm, the constructors: lc_bvar needs i < k'; lc_fvar ✓; lc_app uses IH twice; lc_abs: apply lc_abs; apply IHH; lia; lc_true/false ✓; lc_if uses IH3 times. Let me write:
```coq
Proof.
  intros k k' t Hle H. induction H.
  - apply lc_bvar. lia.
  - apply lc_fvar.
  - apply lc_app; assumption.
  - apply lc_abs. apply IHH. lia.
  - apply lc_true.
  - apply lc_false.
  - apply lc_if; assumption.
Qed.
```


lc_open_rec:
```coq
Lemma lc_open_rec : forall t k u, lc_at (S k) t -> lc_at k u -> lc_at k (open_rec k u t).
Proof.
  induction t; intros k u Hlc Hu; simpl.
  - (* bvar i *) 
    destruct (Nat.eqb k n) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst. exact Hu.
    + apply lc_bvar. inversion Hlc. subst. apply Nat.eqb_neq in E. lia.
  ...
```
Hmm, `open_rec` uses `if Nat.eqb k i then u else tm_bvar i`. Let me write it generically:
- bvar n: simpl. destruct (Nat.eqb k n) eqn:E. If true, goal lc_at k u ✓ Hu. If false: need lc_at k (bvar n) = n < k. Inversion Hlc : lc_at (S k) (bvar n) gives n < S k. And E : Nat.eqb k n = false → k <> n → n ≠ k → n < k (since n < S k means n ≤ k and n ≠ k) ✓ lia.
- fvar ✓.
- app t1 t2: Hlc : lc_at (S k) (app t1 t2) → inversion gives lc_at (S k) t1, lc_at (S k) t2; apply lc_app; [apply IHt1 | apply IHt2] ✓.
- abs T t1: Hlc → lc_at (S (S k)) t1. Goal lc_at k (abs T (open_rec (S k) u t1)) → lc_abs → lc_at (S k) (open_rec (S k) u t1). Apply IHt1 with (k := S k): need lc_at (S (S k)) t1 ✓ and lc_at (S k) u (from Hu via lc_at_mono) ✓.
- true/false ✓.
- if: ✓ three IHs.

The induction is on t with k, u generalized: `induction t; intros k u Hlc Hu` — but t's subterms need the IH at the same t? For abs, the IH is about t1 with any k,u ✓.

lc_open_rec_inv:
```coq
Lemma lc_open_rec_inv : forall t k u, lc_at k (open_rec k u t) -> lc_at (S k) t.

  - apply lc_false.
  - apply lc_if; inversion H; subst; eauto.
```

```coq
Lemma open_rec_lc : forall t j k v, lc_at k t -> k <= j -> open_rec j v t = t.
Proof.

    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst. inversion Hlc. lia.

  - f_equal. apply IHt. inversion Hlc. assumption. lia.
  - reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - repeat f_equal; auto; try (apply IHt1 || apply IHt2 || apply IHt3); auto.
Qed.
```

Now the fundamental theorem structure. Let me write it as:

```coq
Theorem fundamental : forall Gamma t T,
  has_type Gamma t T ->
  forall rho,
    (forall x, locally_closed (rho x)) ->
    (forall x S, Gamma x = Some S -> R S (rho x)) ->
    R T (subst rho t).
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T Hty.
  induction Hty; intros rho Hrho_lc Hrho_c.
  - (* T_Var *) simpl. apply Hrho_c. assumption.
  - (* T_Abs *) 
    simpl.
    apply R_abs_intro.
    + apply lc_subst_typing... (* locally_closed (tm_abs T1 (subst rho t1)) *)
    + (* Hbody *)
      intros v Hv Hrv.
      destruct (fresh_atom L t1) as [x [HxL Hxt]].
      assert (Hlc_ext : forall y, locally_closed (extend rho x v y)).
      { intros y. unfold extend. destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E. 
        - (* y = x *) apply Nat.eqb_eq in E; subst; apply R_lc with (T := T1); exact Hrv.
        - apply Hrho_lc. }
      assert (Hctx_ext : forall y S, update Gamma x T1 y = Some S -> R S (extend rho x v y)).
      { intros y S Hy. unfold update in Hy. destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
        - apply Nat.eqb_eq in E; subst. inversion Hy; subst. unfold extend. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl. exact Hrv.
        - apply Nat.eqb_neq in E. unfold extend. rewrite E. apply Hrho_c. exact Hy. }
      (* apply the IH *)
      specialize (IHHty x HxL (extend rho x v) Hlc_ext Hctx_ext).  (* IH is for `open t1 (fvar x)` *)
      rewrite (subst_open_rec t1 rho x v 0 Hrho_lc Hxt) in IHHty.
      exact IHHty.
  - (* T_App *) simpl. apply R_app with (A := T1). apply IHHty1. assumption. assumption. ... 
```
Hmm, need to be careful with hypothesis names: in T_App, the constructors' arguments: `T_App : forall Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2, has_type Gamma t1 (T1->T2) -> has_type Gamma t2 T1 -> has_type Gamma (tm_app t1 t2) T2`. After induction, IHs: IHHty1 : forall rho, lc-images -> ctx -> R (T1->T2) (subst rho t1); IHHty2 : ... R T1 (subst rho t2). Goal: R T2 (subst rho (tm_app t1 t2)) = R T2 (tm_app (subst rho t1) (subst rho t2)) ✓.
`apply R_app with (A := T1) (t := subst rho t1) (u := subst rho t2)`: R_app : forall A B t u, R (Arrow A B) t -> R A u -> R B (tm_app t u). Goal R T2 (tm_app ...) with T2 = Arrow T1 T2? No wait: in T_App, the result type is T2 and the function type is T1 -> T2. So B := T2, A := T1. `apply R_app with (A := T1) (B := T2)`: goal R T2 (app (subst rho t1) (subst rho t2)) matches R B (app t u) with B = T2 ✓.

Then `apply IHHty1; assumption` etc.

- T_True: R Bool (subst rho tm_true) = SN true ∧ lc true: split; [apply value_SN; apply v_true | apply lc_true].
- T_False ✓.
- T_If: apply R_if with... `simpl; apply R_if with (t1 := subst rho t1) (t2 := subst rho t2) (t3 := subst rho t3); [apply IHHty1 | apply IHHty2 | apply IHHty3]; assumption.`

Wait, T_If's premises: has_type Gamma t1 Bool, has_type Gamma t2 T, has_type Gamma t3 T (T is the result type). IHs accordingly ✓.

```coq
  (forall v, value v -> R A v -> R B (open body v)) ->
```

```coq
Lemma abs_no_step : forall A body s, ~ (tm_abs A body --> s).
```

```coq
(** Free variables and free-variable substitution. *)

Fixpoint occurs (x : atom) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => False
  | tm_fvar y => x = y
  | tm_app t1 t2 => occurs x t1 \/ occurs x t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => occurs x t1
  | tm_true => False
  | tm_false => False
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => occurs x t1 \/ occurs x t2 \/ occurs x t3
  end.

Fixpoint subst (rho : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => rho x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst rho t1) (subst rho t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst rho t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => tm_if (subst rho t1) (subst rho t2) (subst rho t3)
  end.

Definition extend (rho : atom -> tm) (x : atom) (v : tm) : atom -> tm :=
  fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then v else rho y.

Fixpoint max_fvar (t : tm) : nat :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => 0
  | tm_fvar x => x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => Nat.max (max_fvar t1) (max_fvar t2)
  | tm_abs _ t1 => max_fvar t1
  | tm_true => 0
  | tm_false => 0
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => Nat.max (max_fvar t1) (Nat.max (max_fvar t2) (max_fvar t3))
  end.

Fixpoint max_atom_list (L : list atom) : nat :=
  match L with
  | nil => 0
  | cons x L' => Nat.max x (max_atom_list L')
  end.
```

Then lemmas. Let me write them all.

One thing: `Nat.max` vs `max` — in Arith.PeanoNat there's `Nat.max` and `max` notation. PeanoNat defines `max` as Nat.max ✓. I'll use `Nat.max` and lemmas `Nat.le_max_l : n <= Nat.max n m`, `Nat.le_max_r`, and `Nat.max_le_iff` maybe. For `max_fvar_ub`, prove by induction on t using lia ✓.

```coq
Lemma R_abs_app_any : forall A B body,
  locally_closed (tm_abs A body) ->
  (forall v, value v -> R A v -> R B (open body v)) ->
  (forall v, value v -> R A v -> R B (tm_app (tm_abs A body) v)) ->
```

```coq
  (forall t'', t --> t'' -> t'' = t') -> R T t' -> R T t.
```

```coq
Lemma R_stuck : forall T,
  forall t, locally_closed t -> ~ value t ->
  (forall t', t --> t' -> False) -> R T t.
```

- T_Var : forall Gamma x T, Gamma x = Some T -> has_type Gamma (tm_fvar x) T → pattern: Gamma x T H
- T_Abs : forall (L : list atom) Gamma T1 T2 t1, (forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type ...) -> has_type ... → pattern: L Gamma T1 T2 t1 H IH
- T_App : forall Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2, has_type Gamma t1 (T1->T2) -> has_type Gamma t2 T1 -> ... → pattern: Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 H1 IH1 H2 IH2

- T_If : forall Gamma t1 t2 t3 T, ... → Gamma t1 t2 t3 T H1 IH1 H2 IH2 H3 IH3


Now the fundamental theorem. Let me write it:

```coq
Lemma R_subst_lc : ... (typing_lc + subst_lc) — inline.

Theorem fundamental : forall Gamma t T,
  has_type Gamma t T ->
  forall rho,
    (forall x, locally_closed (rho x)) ->
    (forall x S, Gamma x = Some S -> R S (rho x)) ->
    R T (subst rho t).
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T Hty.
  induction Hty as [Gamma0 x T0 HVar
                 | L Gamma0 T1 T2 t1 Hprem IHAbs
                 | Gamma0 t1 t2 T1 T2 H1 IH1 H2 IH2
                 | Gamma0 | Gamma0
                 | Gamma0 t1 t2 t3 T0 H1 IH1 H2 IH2 H3 IH3];
    intros rho Hrl Hrc.
  - (* T_Var *) simpl. apply Hrc. exact HVar.
  - (* T_Abs *) simpl. apply R_abs_intro.
    + (* lc *) apply lc_abs. apply subst_lc.
      * exact Hrl.
      * destruct (fresh_atom L t1) as [xx [HxL _]].
        pose proof (Hprem xx HxL) as Hp.
        apply lc_open_rec_inv with (k := 0) (u := tm_fvar xx) in Hp.
        exact Hp.
    + (* body property *) intros v Hv Hrv.
      destruct (fresh_atom L t1) as [xx [HxL Hxt]].
      assert (Hlce : forall y, locally_closed (extend rho xx v y)).
      { intros y. unfold extend. destruct (Nat.eqb xx y) eqn:E.
        - apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst. apply R_lc with (T := T1). exact Hrv.
        - apply Hrl. }
      assert (Hcte : forall y S, update Gamma0 xx T1 y = Some S -> R S (extend rho xx v y)).
      { intros y S Hy. unfold update in Hy. unfold extend.
        destruct (Nat.eqb xx y) eqn:E.
        - apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst. inversion Hy. subst. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl. exact Hrv.
        - apply Hrc. exact Hy. }
      pose proof (IHAbs xx HxL (extend rho xx v) Hlce Hcte) as Hb.
      rewrite (subst_open_rec t1 rho xx v 0 Hrl Hxt) in Hb.
      exact Hb.
  - (* T_App *) simpl. apply R_app with (A := T1) (B := T2).
    + apply IH1; assumption.
    + apply IH2; assumption.
  - (* T_True *) simpl. split.
    + apply value_SN. apply v_true.
    + apply lc_true.
  - (* T_False *) simpl. split.
    + apply value_SN. apply v_false.
    + apply lc_false.
  - (* T_If *) simpl. apply R_if.
    + apply IH1; assumption.
    + apply IH2; assumption.
    + apply IH3; assumption.
Qed.
```

Concerns:
1. In T_Abs, the `simpl` might not reduce `subst rho (tm_abs T1 t1)` if the notation interferes. Should be OK.
